Topic: a story about an unusual holiday trip

Text:
By Mark Carter

Last summer, my friend Tom had a strange suggestion: “Why don’t we stay in a cave for our holiday?” Normally, we go to hotels, but I was curious. We packed our own food and a small stove. The cave was inside a quiet hill, far from the city.

The owner of the cave, Mr. Lee, showed us around. He said, “You can cook inside if you’re careful.” We tried his method—lighting a fire outside and bringing food in. It worked, but after two days, I got a bad stomach from the cold air.

Tom had a question: “Is this safe?” Mr. Lee laughed and said, “Don’t worry! Many people do it.” The trip wasn’t perfect, but the informal atmosphere made it fun.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Mark and Tom stay in a cave instead of a hotel?
Correct: Their friend suggested it as an unusual holiday idea.
Distractors:
1. They wanted to save money by staying in free accommodation. / They were afraid of hotels because of past bad experiences. / The hotel they booked was fully booked, so they had no choice.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text’s focus on Tom’s suggestion. Distractor 1 assumes cost; distractor 2 misinterprets “normally” as dislike; distractor 3 introduces unsupported logic.
---
Q2: What problem did Mark have during the trip?
Correct: He suffered from stomach pain because of the cold air.
Distractors:
1. He got lost on the way to the cave. / He broke his leg climbing the hill. / He missed his family and wanted to go home.
Explanation:
The correct answer directly quotes “bad stomach.” Distractor 1 ignores the clear route description; distractor 2 confuses “hill” with injury; distractor 3 misreads the focus on physical discomfort.
---
Q3: How did Mr. Lee respond to Tom’s safety question?
Correct: He reassured them that staying there was safe for many people.
Distractors:
1. He told them to leave immediately because it was dangerous. / He said they should have brought warmer clothes. / He ignored Tom and kept talking about the cave’s history.
Explanation:
The correct answer paraphrases “Don’t worry!” Distractor 1 contradicts the reassurance; distractor 2 misattributes advice about clothes; distractor 3 misinterprets “laughed” as indifference.



Topic: a story about an unusual family celebration

Text:
By Lena Carter

Every year, my family celebrates National Left-Handers Day—completely different from others! We had a variety of fun things: a quiz, silly games, and even writing essays about our favorite left-handed heroes.

His favorite part was decorating with fairy lights. My sister argued that we should add a dance, but neither of us expected the deep laughter when we tried to clap with our left hands!

We changed direction at midnight and sang off-key. Now, it’s our most special tradition.

Questions:
Q1: Why was the family’s celebration unusual?
Correct: Because they celebrated a holiday most people don’t know about.
Distractors:
1. They had a party with no decorations.
2. Only right-handed people were invited.
3. They argued the whole time without laughing.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text’s mention of an unusual holiday. Distractors 1–3 contradict details (lights were used, left-handers were celebrated, they laughed).
---
Q2: What did the family do that made them laugh?
Correct: They tried to clap with their left hands and couldn’t do it well.
Distractors:
1. They wrote essays about right-handed people.
2. They danced perfectly in time.
3. They argued about who was the best left-hander.
Explanation:
The correct answer aligns with the text’s description of clumsy clapping. Distractors 1–3 misrepresent the essay topic, dancing quality, and argument focus.
---
Q3: How did the celebration end?
Correct: They sang badly together at midnight.
Distractors:
1. They went to bed early without singing.
2. They wrote more essays until dawn.
3. They argued until someone cried.
Explanation:
The correct answer reflects the text’s "sang off-key" at midnight. Distractors 1–3 ignore the singing or exaggerate conflict.



Topic: a description of a morning routine that makes me happy

Text:
"My morning routine makes my heart happy! In general, I wake up early and take a step outside. The fresh air is wonderful, and I reach the lake near my home. I love to walk there and help protect the nature around it. Sometimes, I see a bird or even a celebrity walking their dog—it’s amazing! When I arrive, the view is so beautiful that I say, ‘Wow!’ Everything feels all right, and my day starts with joy."

Questions:
Q1: What does the writer do first in their morning routine?
Correct: They go outside for fresh air.
Distractors:
1. They wake up and drink coffee. / They call a friend. / They run to the lake.
Explanation:
The text says, "I wake up early and take a step outside." The correct answer is "They go outside for fresh air." The other options are wrong because the text does not mention coffee, calling a friend, or running. A weak reader might choose these because they assume morning routines include coffee or exercise.
---
Q2: Why does the writer say, “Wow!”?
Correct: The view of the lake is beautiful.
Distractors:
1. They see a celebrity. / They finish their morning exercise. / The weather is perfect.
Explanation:
The text says, "When I arrive, the view is so beautiful that I say, ‘Wow!’" The correct answer is "The view of the lake is beautiful." The other options are wrong because "Wow" refers to the view, not just the celebrity or weather. A weak reader might pick these if they focus only on the celebrity line or assume "wow" applies to everything.
---
Q3: What makes the writer’s morning special?
Correct: They see unexpected things like birds or celebrities.
Distractors:
1. They protect the lake every day. / They have a long walk. / They feel tired but happy.
Explanation:
The text says, "Sometimes, I see a bird or even a celebrity... it’s amazing!" The correct answer is "They see unexpected things like birds or celebrities." The other options are wrong because protecting the lake is part of the routine, not the special moment, and the text does not mention tiredness. A weak reader might choose these if they confuse the general routine with the special highlight.



Topic: a funny story that happened to my friends and me

Text:
By Alex Carter

Last summer, my friends and I went camping with my twin brother, Jake. We brought a double amount of food, but my friend Leo only wanted sweet snacks. He said he was a big fan of a celebrity who only ate candy!

At dinner, we all sat around the fire, but Leo’s plate was full of chocolate bars. Jake and I started an argument because we were starving, and our food was disappearing! Leo’s character made it worse—he acted like a movie star, saying, “I don’t eat boring meals!”

We laughed and chatted for hours, but the next day, our parents said the food increased in price because we wasted so much. Now, we always bring extra snacks… just in case!

Questions:
Q1: Why did Leo only eat sweet snacks at the camp?
Correct: He was copying a celebrity who only ate candy.
Distractors:
1. He didn’t like the main meal because it was too salty.
2. He was on a diet and only ate healthy snacks.
3. His parents told him to eat only desserts.
Explanation:
The text says Leo ate sweet snacks because he admired a celebrity who only ate candy. The correct answer matches this. The other options are wrong because:
- Salty food is never mentioned.
- Diet/healthy snacks contradicts "chocolate bars."
- Parents’ instructions are not referenced.
---
Q2: What made the twins angry during dinner?
Correct: They were hungry because Leo ate all their food.
Distractors:
1. Leo refused to share his snacks with them.
2. They didn’t like his celebrity impersonations.
3. Their plates were dirty, and they had to clean up.
Explanation:
The twins argued because they were starving and Leo ate their food. The correct answer reflects this. The distractors are wrong because:
- Refusing to share is not stated—Leo ate his own snacks.
- Celebrity impersonations were funny, not the cause of the argument.
- Dirty plates are irrelevant—the issue was food disappearance.
---
Q3: What happened because of the wasted food?
Correct: Their parents said the food cost more because they wasted so much.
Distractors:
1. They had to buy more food at the store.
2. The twins blamed Leo and stopped talking to him.
3. They decided never to go camping again.
Explanation:
The text explains that the price increased due to waste. The correct answer paraphrases this. The distractors are wrong because:
- Buying more food is not mentioned—the issue was cost, not quantity.
- Stopping talking is false; they laughed and chatted.
- Never camping again is exaggerated—they still go camping (just with extra snacks).



Topic: an adventure story about getting lost in the forest

Text:
Leo’s Lost Adventure
By Emma Carter

Leo loves autumn (season) in the forest, but today he feels nervous. His dad takes him on a walk, and Leo brings equipment: a map, a flashlight, and water. "Stay direct," his dad says. They walk for hours but almost miss a turn. Suddenly, they are lost!

Leo remembers his dad’s rule: "If you need help, follow the network of trails." He looks down—his torn sock is caught on a branch! Behind it, he sees old notes written in pencil. "This is mine!" he laughs. The notes lead to a ranger’s cabin.

Inside, Leo finds a gift: a compass left by another lost child. "You’re clever!" his dad says. Leo smiles. The forest wasn’t so scary after all.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Leo’s torn sock help him?
Correct: It revealed hidden explorer notes behind the branch.
Distractors:
1. It led him to water.
2. It had a secret message written on it.
3. It was a gift from his dad.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text’s detail about the sock showing notes. Distractor 1 is wrong because the river is mentioned but not linked to the sock. Distractor 2 is wrong because no message is described. Distractor 3 is wrong because the sock is Leo’s, not a gift. A weak reader might assume the sock had a magical purpose (distractors 1–2) or confuse it with the later gift (distractor 3).
---
Q2: What did Leo find in the ranger’s cabin, and why was it important?
Correct: A compass that belonged to another lost child, which helped them navigate.
Distractors:
1. A first-aid kit to treat his cut foot.
2. A book about forest animals.
3. A whistle to call for help.
Explanation:
The correct answer paraphrases the compass and its purpose. Distractor 1 is wrong because the text doesn’t mention injuries or first aid. Distractor 2 is wrong because the gift is functional (compass), not informational. Distractor 3 is wrong because the compass is the tool used, not a whistle. A weak reader might focus on "lost" and assume medical needs (1) or overgeneralize "gift" (2–3).
---
Q3: How did Leo’s dad help him stay safe in the forest?
Correct: He gave Leo a map and told him to stay on direct paths.
Distractors:
1. He carried Leo on his back.
2. He sang songs to keep Leo calm.
3. He promised to buy Leo ice cream if they returned safely.
Explanation:
The correct answer combines the dad’s advice ("Stay direct") and Leo’s preparation (map). Distractor 1 is wrong because the text doesn’t describe physical help. Distractor 2 is wrong because no singing is mentioned. Distractor 3 is wrong because the text doesn’t mention rewards. A weak reader might assume parental actions are more hands-on (1) or emotional (2–3) rather than advisory.



Topic: an adventure story about finding a secret door in the house

Text:
By Myself

I love reading novels about secret doors. One day, I visited my uncle’s old house. It was weak in some places, but I wanted to explore. Suddenly, I heard a strange noise behind a painting. I pulled it, and there was a hidden door!

Inside, I saw a cartoon of a businessman and recognized him—it was the house’s old owner! I looked for evidence and found a key under a loose floorboard. My uncle said his family had a special relationship with this house. I felt lucky to discover it. Now, I’m creative with stories about the past!

Questions:
Q1: Why did the narrator feel lucky?
Correct: They discovered a hidden door in the house.
Distractors:
1. They won a prize in a writing competition.
2. They recognized a famous cartoon character.
3. Their uncle gave them a new key.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches “I felt lucky to discover it” (the door). Distractor 1 is false (no competition). Distractor 2 is false (the cartoon was of a businessman). Distractor 3 is false (the key was found, not given).
---
Q2: What did the narrator find under the loose floorboard?
Correct: A key that connected to the house’s past.
Distractors:
1. A map of the house.
2. A note from the businessman.
3. A broken cartoon.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches “I found a key under a loose floorboard.” Distractor 1 is false (no map). Distractor 2 is false (no note). Distractor 3 is false (the cartoon was intact).
---
Q3: How did the cartoon help the narrator understand the house’s history?
Correct: It showed the house’s former owner, linking it to the family’s history.
Distractors:
1. It had a message written on it.
2. It was a clue to another hidden room.
3. It was a copy of a famous painting.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches “I saw a cartoon of a businessman and recognized him—it was the house’s old owner!” Distractor 1 is false (no message). Distractor 2 is false (no other room). Distractor 3 is false (not a famous painting).



Topic: an adventure story about finding a treasure

Text:
By Jake Brown

Last summer, I found an old newspaper in my grandpa’s attic. It had an advertising for a treasure hidden by King Richard long ago. The note said the treasure was near a stone column by the sea.

One day, I walked near the old pier and saw a strange round rock. Inside it was a map and a small piece of paper in my pocket! It said, “Dig where the waves meet the sort of rocks.”

My friend Sam said, “Let’s go now!” We followed the clues but almost got caught in a big wave. At the final spot, Sam suggested we dig carefully. Under the sand, we found a chest! Now, the treasure is our possession.

Questions:
Q1: What did Jake find in the newspaper?
Correct: An advertisement about a treasure hidden by a king.
Distractors:
1. A map showing where the treasure was buried.
2. A story about pirates who lost their gold.
3. A list of old kings and their possessions.

Explanation:
The correct answer matches “an advertising for a treasure hidden by King Richard.” Distractor 1 is wrong because the map was inside the rock. Distractor 2 is wrong because the text mentions a king, not pirates. Distractor 3 is wrong because the newspaper had an advertisement, not a list.
---
Q2: What did the note inside the rock say?
Correct: They should dig where the waves meet a certain type of rock.
Distractors:
1. The treasure was buried under the stone column.
2. They needed to follow the waves to the final clue.
3. The king’s name was written on the back.

Explanation:
The correct answer matches “Dig where the waves meet the sort of rocks.” Distractor 1 is wrong because the column was a landmark, not the digging spot. Distractor 2 is misleading—it says dig where waves meet rocks, not just follow waves. Distractor 3 is wrong because no name was mentioned.
---
Q3: How did Jake and Sam finally find the treasure?
Correct: They dug carefully at the final spot indicated by the clues.
Distractors:
1. They found the treasure by following the waves to the column.
2. Sam already knew where it was hidden.
3. They saw the chest when a big wave washed it ashore.

Explanation:
The correct answer matches “At the final spot, Sam suggested we dig carefully.” Distractor 1 is wrong because the column was not the digging spot. Distractor 2 is wrong because Sam’s suggestion did not imply prior knowledge. Distractor 3 is wrong because they dug it up, not found it washed ashore.



Topic: a story about a day when everything went wrong but ended well

Text:
By Jake Miller

It was a lovely autumn season, but my day started badly. I woke up with a terrible illness—a headache and a fever. I was usually very active, but now I felt weak. I had an important task at work, so I decided to go anyway.

I drove too fast and got lost. My car stopped suddenly near a quiet bar. I needed help, but my phone had no battery. Just then, a kind retired soldier walked in. He was actually a mechanic! He fixed my car quickly.

By the end, I was late, but the day ended in a lovely way. The soldier even gave me a cup of tea. Everything worked out!

Questions:
Q1: Why did Jake go to work even though he was sick?
Correct: He had an important task to complete.
Distractors:
1. He didn’t want to miss his favorite season.
2. His boss told him to go.
3. He needed money for a new car.

Explanation:
The text says, "I had an important task at work, so I decided to go anyway." The correct answer matches this. Distractor 1 is wrong because season is mentioned but not the reason for going to work. Distractor 2 is wrong because no boss is mentioned. Distractor 3 is wrong because no financial need is implied.
---
Q2: What happened when Jake’s car stopped near the bar?
Correct: A retired soldier helped him fix his car.
Distractors:
1. A stranger gave him money for gas.
2. He walked home because it wasn’t far.
3. The bar owner called a tow truck.

Explanation:
The text says, "A kind retired soldier... fixed my car quickly." The correct answer matches this. Distractor 1 is wrong because no money or stranger is mentioned. Distractor 2 is wrong because he couldn’t walk (car broke down). Distractor 3 is wrong because no tow truck is mentioned.
---
Q3: How did Jake’s day end?
Correct: He received help and felt happy about the outcome.
Distractors:
1. He arrived late but got a promotion.
2. His illness got worse.
3. He had to stay at the bar overnight.

Explanation:
The text says, "The day ended in a lovely way... Everything worked out!" The correct answer matches this. Distractor 1 is wrong because no promotion is mentioned. Distractor 2 is wrong because his illness improved. Distractor 3 is wrong because no overnight stay is mentioned.



Topic: a story about a time I helped a neighbor

Text:
By Maria Lopez

Recently, I helped a neighbor who was a businessman. His office had a problem: thick smoke came from a broken heater, and the noise rate was too high. He sent me an invitation to his office because he trusted me.

At first, I couldn’t figure out the issue, but he gave me a suggestion: call a technician. I decided to act quickly. The key factor was his tight schedule, so I worked fast. After an hour, the problem was fixed! He was very happy and thanked me.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the businessman send Maria an invitation to his office?
Correct: Because he trusted her to solve the problem.
Distractors:
1. She was his employee.
2. She had fixed his heater before.
3. He needed help with paperwork.
Explanation:
The text says, "He trusted me" (not employee/boss relationship). "Couldn’t figure it out" shows she didn’t fix it before. The problem was smoke/noise, not paperwork.
---
Q2: What was the most important reason Maria could help him quickly?
Correct: His tight schedule made her work fast.
Distractors:
1. She knew how to fix heaters.
2. The technician arrived very fast.
3. She wore thick gloves to work safely.
Explanation:
The text states "The key factor was his tight schedule" (not her skills or technician speed). "Thick" describes smoke, not gloves.
---
Q3: How did Maria feel about helping her neighbor at the end?
Correct: She was happy because the problem was solved.
Distractors:
1. She was angry because it took too long.
2. She felt nothing because he didn’t pay her.
3. She was scared of the thick smoke.
Explanation:
The text implies happiness ("He was very happy and thanked me"). "Fixed after an hour" isn’t described as slow. No mention of payment or fear.



Topic: a story about my favorite toy from childhood

Text:
By Maria Lopez

When I was little, my favorite toy was an incredible wooden train set. It had a long track, and I loved pretending I was a passenger riding on it. One day, I was playing outside when the train almost fell off the table. I grabbed it immediately before it hit the ground!

My dad helped me build the track again, and we even made a small station with a toy stamp for tickets. I wanted to publish a book about my train adventures, but my mom said I was too young. Once, I almost cut my finger with a knife while cutting paper for the train station—ah, that was scary!

The best part was the process of playing with it every day. Now, I still remember how much fun it was!

Questions:
Q1: What did Maria almost drop when she was playing outside?
Correct: She almost dropped her wooden train set.
Distractors:
1. Her toy stamp collection.
2. A passenger figure from the train.
3. Her father’s knife.
Explanation:
The text says, "the train almost fell off the table." The correct answer refers to the train. Distractor 1 is wrong because the stamp was part of the station, not dropped. Distractor 2 is wrong because the passenger was imaginary. Distractor 3 is wrong because the knife was used later indoors, not dropped outside.
---
Q2: Why did Maria want to publish something about her toy?
Correct: She wanted to write a book about her train adventures.
Distractors:
1. To sell the train set and buy a new one.
2. Because her mom asked her to.
3. To teach other children how to build tracks.
Explanation:
The text says, "I wanted to publish a book about my train adventures." Distractor 1 is wrong because selling isn’t mentioned. Distractor 2 is wrong because her mom said she was too young, not that she asked. Distractor 3 is wrong because teaching isn’t mentioned—it’s about personal fun.
---
Q3: What almost happened when Maria was making the train station?
Correct: She almost cut her finger with a knife.
Distractors:
1. She fell off the chair while cutting.
2. The knife broke into pieces.
3. She dropped the stamp on the floor.
Explanation:
The text says, "Once, I almost cut my finger with a knife..." Distractor 1 is wrong because falling isn’t mentioned. Distractor 2 is wrong because the knife wasn’t broken. Distractor 3 is wrong because the stamp incident isn’t linked to the knife.



Topic: a story about my first day at school

Text:
By Maria Lopez

My parents are divorced, but I live with my mum. Every morning, I wash my hands and write in my diary. Today was my first day at a new school!

I was nervous, but my teacher was kind. First, I fell down in the hallway, but I didn’t cry. Then, I hid behind a tree when I saw a big dog. After that, we did an experiment with colours. It was fun!

At lunch, we served food to our friends. Later, we played football with our coach. He was very nice and helped us learn the rules.

When I got home, I wrote in my diary: "Today was exciting!"

Questions:
Q1: Why was Maria nervous on her first day at school?
Correct: Because it was her first day at a new school.
Distractors:
1. Because her parents were divorced.
2. Because she didn’t like her teacher.
3. Because she fell down in the hallway.

Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text ("first day at a new school"). The other options are wrong because:
- Divorce is background info, not the reason for nerves.
- The teacher was kind, not disliked.
- Falling happened after she was nervous, not because of it.
---
Q2: What did Maria do after she fell down in the hallway?
Correct: She hid behind a tree when she saw a big dog.
Distractors:
1. She wrote in her diary.
2. She served lunch to her friends.
3. She played football with her coach.

Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text ("Then, I hid behind a tree..."). The other options are wrong because:
- Writing in the diary happened before and after school, not immediately after falling.
- Serving lunch and playing football happened later in the day.
---
Q3: What did Maria write in her diary at the end of the day?
Correct: She wrote that the day was exciting.
Distractors:
1. She wrote that she fell down.
2. She wrote that her teacher was mean.
3. She wrote that she didn’t like school.

Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text ("Today was exciting!"). The other options are wrong because:
- Falling was not mentioned in her diary.
- The teacher was kind, not mean.
- She said the day was exciting, not that she didn’t like school.



Topic: a story about a best friend I met when I was little

Text:
My Best Friend from Childhood

When I was little, I met my best friend in a big department store. My mum and I were looking for a toy, and I saw a boy climbing the stairs near the printed posters. He seemed shy at first, but then he smiled and asked if I wanted to race him down!

We played together for hours. He was very clever—he could fix broken toys and even solve puzzles faster than me! According to my mum, he loved science books and said he wanted to be an engineer when he grew up.

One day, he gave me some cash he had saved to buy a small gift. I was so happy! Before he left, we promised to meet again. I still have a printed photo of us together. It was a chance I’ll never forget.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the narrator keep a printed photo of their friend?
Correct: To remember their special time together.
Distractors:
1. Because the photo was very colorful.
2. To give it to his family later.
3. Because his mother asked him to.
Explanation:
The text says, "I still have a printed photo of us together." The correct answer refers to keeping the photo as a memory. Option 1 is wrong because the text doesn’t mention color. Option 2 is wrong because the narrator keeps it, not shares it. Option 3 is wrong because the mother isn’t mentioned in this context.
---
Q2: What did the boy’s mother say about his future plans?
Correct: She mentioned that he loved science books and wanted to be an engineer.
Distractors:
1. She said he would travel the world.
2. She told him to study harder in school.
3. She said he should be a doctor instead.
Explanation:
The text states, "According to my mum, he loved science books and said he wanted to be an engineer." The correct answer matches this exactly. Option 1 is wrong because travel isn’t mentioned. Option 2 is wrong because the mother doesn’t give advice. Option 3 is wrong because the text specifies engineer, not doctor.
---
Q3: How did the two friends spend most of their time together?
Correct: They played games and fixed toys.
Distractors:
1. They read books in the store.
2. They climbed stairs and looked at posters.
3. They saved cash for a big gift.
Explanation:
The text says, "We played together for hours... He could fix broken toys." The correct answer summarizes their main activities. Option 1 is wrong because they played, not read. Option 2 is wrong because climbing stairs was just one moment, not their usual activity. Option 3 is wrong because only the boy gave cash, not both friends.



Topic: a story about a birthday party I will never forget

Text:
By Mia Carter

Last year, I turned 12, and my parents let me plan my own birthday party. I was the director—but things didn’t go as planned! First, the balloons started flying everywhere, and my pet dog, Max, stole the cake! My heart sank when my little brother fell asleep on the couch during the games. It was general chaos, but my friend Jake—who has a funny personality—made sure we all laughed. We involved everyone in silly races, and even my shy cousin joined. To remember the day, we made a record of the party with photos. It was messy, but it was necessary for the best memories. I’ll never forget that birthday!

Questions:
Q1: Why did Mia say her birthday party was "necessary"?
Correct: Because it created the best memories, even though it was chaotic.
Distractors:
1. It was necessary because the party was very quiet.
2. It was necessary because they had to follow a strict plan.
3. It was necessary because the guests were unhappy.
Explanation:
The text says, "It was messy, but it was necessary for the best memories." The correct answer matches this. Distractor 1 is wrong because the party was chaotic (balloons flying, dog stole cake). Distractor 2 is wrong because Mia was the director but things "didn’t go as planned." Distractor 3 is wrong because the guests "all laughed."
---
Q2: What happened that made Mia’s heart sink?
Correct: Her little brother fell asleep during the games.
Distractors:
1. Her little brother broke a plate.
2. The cake was burnt.
3. Her pet dog ran away.
Explanation:
The text says, "My heart sank when my little brother fell asleep on the couch." Distractor 1 is wrong because there’s no mention of broken plates. Distractor 2 is wrong because the cake was stolen by the dog, not burnt. Distractor 3 is wrong because the dog was present ("my pet dog Max").
---
Q3: How did Jake help during the party?
Correct: He made sure everyone laughed and joined the activities.
Distractors:
1. He made sure everyone left on time.
2. He cooked the cake.
3. He played music loudly.
Explanation:
The text says, "my friend Jake... made sure we all laughed. We involved everyone in silly races." The correct answer matches this. Distractor 1 is wrong because there’s no mention of timing. Distractor 2 is wrong because the cake was store-bought (dog stole it). Distractor 3 is wrong because no music is mentioned.



Topic: a story about helping a lost pet find its home

Text:
By Alex Carter

My dog Max was lost for three days! I was very worried. A nice guy from my street suggested checking under the neighbor’s couch. Sure enough, Max was hiding under the carpet! He was dirty but happy to see me.

I used his training to call him back. I gave him a large quantity of treats, including his favorite chicken flavor. I also called the vet, just in case. The reunion was incredible—Max jumped on me and licked my face!

There was a funny part too. Max’s collar was broken, so I had to replace it with a new one. My neighbor laughed and said, “This is like a comedy show!” The benefit of training was clear—Max knew his name and came when I called.

Now, Max sleeps on my bed every night. I’m so glad he’s home!

Questions:
Q1: Why did Max’s owner think training was helpful?
Correct: Because Max knew his name and came when called.
Distractors:
1. Max could find his way home alone. / Wrong: Text says owner called him back, not that he navigated alone.
2. The training helped Max hide better. / Wrong: Training helped him return, not hide.
3. Max’s collar was trained to light up. / Wrong: Collar was replaced, not trained.
Explanation: The text states, “The benefit of training was clear—Max knew his name and came when I called.” This shows training helped with recall, not navigation or hiding. Distractors 1 and 2 misinterpret the purpose of training, while 3 confuses "training" with "technology."
---
Q2: What did the neighbor say that made the owner laugh?
Correct: He said it was like a comedy show.
Distractors:
1. He said Max was a funny dog. / Wrong: The humor was in the situation, not Max’s personality.
2. He said the owner should train Max more. / Wrong: Neighbor laughed about the reunion, not training.
3. He said Max was lost for too long. / Wrong: Text doesn’t mention criticism of the time lost.
Explanation: The text quotes, “This is like a comedy show!” The neighbor’s comment refers to the circumstances (reunion + broken collar), not Max’s behavior or the owner’s actions. Distractors 1 and 2 misattribute the joke’s target.
---

2. Behind the owner’s house. / Wrong: Search was at the neighbor’s home, not the owner’s.

Explanation: The text clearly states, “A nice guy... suggested checking under the neighbor’s couch.” Distractors 1 and 2 confuse locations, while 3 introduces an unrelated setting. Weak readers might assume pets hide in open or commercial spaces.

Topic: a story about a traditional festival in my country

Text:
By Linh Nguyen

Tết is the most important festival in Vietnam. Every family prepares for it since January or February. The whole unit—parents, children, and grandparents—works together. Women usually cook a variety of traditional dishes like sticky rice and spring rolls. They also clean the house to avoid bad luck.

A typical Tết activity is visiting relatives. People avoid sweeping on the first day because it might "sweep away" good fortune. Some families hang heavy decorations to keep evil spirits away. Fireworks scare off insects like mice, which symbolize bad luck.

Many people research old traditions to keep them alive. A common question is: "Why do we give red envelopes?" The answer is simple—red means happiness. Even though Tết has changed since ancient times, families still celebrate together.

Questions:
Q1: What is one reason families clean their houses before Tết?
Correct: To remove bad luck before the festival starts.
Distractors:
1. To make the house look nice for guests.
2. To prepare space for new decorations.
3. To avoid stepping on insects.
Explanation:
The text says, "They also clean the house to avoid bad luck." The correct answer explains this superstition. Option 1 is wrong because the text doesn’t mention appearance. Option 2 is incorrect because cleaning isn’t about storage. Option 3 is wrong because insects are linked to fireworks, not cleaning.
---
Q2: Why do some people hang heavy decorations during Tết?
Correct: To keep evil spirits away from the home.
Distractors:
1. To make the house look colorful.
2. To weigh down the doors so they don’t open easily.
3. To attract good luck directly.
Explanation:
The text states, "Some families hang heavy decorations to keep evil spirits away." The correct answer matches this. Option 1 ignores the symbolic purpose. Option 2 misinterprets "weight" literally. Option 3 is incomplete because decorations repel evil, not just bring luck.
---
Q3: How do fireworks help during Tết?
Correct: They scare away pests like mice.
Distractors:
1. They create beautiful light shows for children.
2. They burn away old bad luck.
3. They keep people awake to celebrate longer.
Explanation:
The text explains, "Fireworks scare off insects like mice." The correct answer focuses on pest control. Option 1 is true but irrelevant to the helpful purpose. Option 2 confuses fireworks with burning rituals. Option 3 introduces an unrelated detail.

"The Great Kitchen Accident"

Topic: a story about learning to ride a bike

Text:
By Leo Green

When I was eight, I wanted to ride a bike like a chef balancing plates! My father was my trainer. He said, "Don’t worry, Leo. You’ll learn!"

The first day was hard. The hill near our house was extreme, and the temperature was too hot. I wobbled and fell. My father laughed and said, "Try again!"

I practiced near the lake. The path was smooth, but I forgot the direction and rode into a tree! My father helped me get up. "Push yourself, Leo," he said. "You’re such a beginner, but you’ll get better!"

One day, I tried to ride through the gate in our garden. I almost made it—then crash! I was on the ground again. My father smiled. "An electric bike would be easier, but you need to learn first."

I kept trying. Soon, I rode all the way to the park without falling!

Q1: Why did Leo’s father say, "Don’t worry, Leo. You’ll learn!"?
Correct: Because Leo was nervous about learning to ride a bike.
Distractors:
1. Because Leo had already fallen many times before. / Wrong: The text says he fell that day, not before.
2. Because his father was a bike trainer and knew it would be easy. / Wrong: The text doesn’t say his father was a professional trainer.
3. Because Leo’s bike was broken and needed fixing. / Wrong: The text doesn’t mention a broken bike.

Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text’s implication that Leo needed encouragement. Distractors 1 and 2 misread details, while 3 is unsupported.
---
Q2: What made Leo fall when he was riding near the lake?
Correct: He forgot which way to go (the direction).
Distractors:
1. The path was too bumpy. / Wrong: The text says the path was smooth.
2. He crashed into his father. / Wrong: He rode into a tree, not his father.
3. The temperature was too hot. / Wrong: The heat made him wobble, but he fell because of the direction.

Explanation:
The correct answer directly quotes the text ("forgot the direction"). Distractors 1 and 2 contradict the text, while 3 misinterprets cause and effect.
---

Correct: Because it would help him balance without pedaling so hard.
Distractors:
1. Because electric bikes are faster. / Wrong: The text doesn’t mention speed.
2. Because Leo was too tired to pedal. / Wrong: The text doesn’t say Leo was tired.
3. Because his father wanted to buy him a new bike. / Wrong: The text says Leo needs to learn first.

Explanation:
The correct answer paraphrases the text’s idea of easier balance. Distractors 1 and 2 introduce unsupported ideas, while 3 misrepresents the father’s intention.



Topic: a story about a funny mistake I made as a kid

Text:
My Funny Baseball Mistake

When I was seven, I played baseball in a valley near my house. One day, I tried to push a ball with all my strength, but I lost my balance and fell to the bottom of a small hill. I landed in a big web—it was a spider’s home!

The speed of my fall made the web shake, and I couldn’t move. My legs were stuck, and I felt very uncomfortable. Suddenly, I saw a tiny spider coming toward me. I thought, "Oh no, a fight!" But then I laughed because the spider was smaller than my little finger.

The effect of my mistake was funny—I couldn’t run fast, and my friends had to pull me out. My dad said, "You don’t always need to push so hard!" Now, I know that sometimes, slow and comfortable is better than fast and silly.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the boy fall into the spider’s web?
Correct: He lost his balance while trying to push the baseball too hard.
Distractors:
1. He was running too fast and tripped.
2. The spider attacked him first.
3. He was trying to climb a hill and slipped.
Explanation:
The text says, "I tried to push a ball with all my strength, but I lost my balance." The correct answer matches this. Option 1 is wrong because the boy pushed the ball, not ran. Option 2 is wrong because the spider came after the fall. Option 3 is wrong because he played baseball, not climbed.
---
Q2: What happened after he fell into the web?
Correct: He got stuck in the web and saw a tiny spider coming toward him.
Distractors:
1. His friends helped him immediately.
2. He screamed and ran away.
3. The web broke because he was too heavy.
Explanation:
The text describes: "I saw a tiny spider coming toward me." The correct answer matches this. Option 1 is wrong because friends helped later. Option 2 is wrong because he laughed, not screamed. Option 3 is wrong because the web shaked, not broke.
---
Q3: What lesson did the boy learn from his mistake?
Correct: He realized that sometimes it’s better to be slow and careful than to rush.
Distractors:
1. He should never play baseball in a valley again.
2. Spiders are dangerous, so he should be more careful.
3. He needs to practice pushing harder in baseball.
Explanation:
The text says, "You don’t always need to push so hard!" The correct answer matches this lesson. Option 1 is wrong because the text doesn’t say he stopped playing. Option 2 is wrong because the lesson was about speed, not spiders. Option 3 is wrong because his dad told him not to push harder.



Topic: a story about a bird that visited my window

Text:
A Mysterious Visitor
Every morning, a small bird visited my window. It was curious and always stepped carefully onto the sill. I wondered about its purpose—maybe it liked the heat from the sun or the smells from my cooking.

One day, I opened the window to lift the screen, and the bird watched me. It had a big stomach, so I left some breadcrumbs. I felt like a detective, trying to solve why it came. Maybe it was successful at finding food here!

At night, I imagined the bird giving me a lecture about my messy kitchen. It was funny! Even the petrol in my car seemed less important than this tiny visitor.

Correct: Amused and curious about the bird’s habits.
Distractors:
1. Frustrated because the bird made noise at dawn.
2. Worried the bird was lost or hurt.
3. Bored because the bird didn’t do anything interesting.
Explanation:
The text describes the bird as "curious" and the narrator’s imagination as "funny." The correct answer fits this. Distractor 1 is wrong because noise isn’t mentioned. Distractor 2 is wrong because the bird seemed healthy. Distractor 3 is wrong because the narrator found it interesting.

Topic: a story about my grandparents' old house

Text:
My grandparents lived in an enormous house with a red roof. Every morning, Grandmother would pet her old cat while making breakfast. Grandfather would pull the heavy door open carefully because it always made a loud noise.

The house had beautiful floral patterns on the curtains, but the electricity was old. Sometimes, the lights would flicker, giving me a headache! Grandmother said, "We must fix the wiring—it’s not safe!" But Grandfather disagreed. "This house has a code we can’t change!"

Sadly, they argued about selling it. I remember Grandmother washing dishes with homemade soap, laughing as she told stories. Now, the house is empty, and I miss those days.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Grandfather not want to fix the wiring?
Correct: He believed the house’s old wiring couldn’t be changed.
Distractors:
1. He was afraid of the electrical code. / Wrong – The text says he disagreed, not that he was afraid.
2. He thought the house was too old to repair. / Wrong – He didn’t say it was too old, just that it had a code.
3. He wanted to keep the house exactly as it was. / Wrong – He disagreed with fixing it, but the text doesn’t say he wanted it unchanged forever.

Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text’s exact wording ("This house has a code we can’t change!"). Distractor 1 misinterprets "code" as danger. Distractor 2 assumes "code" means impossible to fix, but the text only says Grandfather disagreed. Distractor 3 overgeneralizes his resistance into a refusal to ever change anything.
---
Q2: What did Grandmother do every morning?
Correct: She took care of her cat and prepared breakfast.
Distractors:
1. She cleaned the entire house. / Wrong – The text only mentions breakfast and petting the cat.
2. She made soap for the family. / Wrong – Soap-making is a memory, not a daily routine.
3. She argued with Grandfather about the wiring. / Wrong – Arguing happened later, not every morning.

Explanation:
The correct answer directly quotes the text ("pet her old cat while making breakfast"). Distractor 1 assumes "making breakfast" = full cleaning. Distractor 2 confuses soap-making (a nostalgic detail) with morning habits. Distractor 3 misplaces the argument in time.
---
Q3: How did the house make the narrator feel?
Correct: The narrator felt sad when thinking about the empty house.
Distractors:
1. The narrator was scared of the flickering lights. / Wrong – The lights gave a headache, not fear.
2. The narrator loved the old wiring. / Wrong – The wiring was a problem, not something loved.
3. The narrator missed the noisy door. / Wrong – The door was annoying, not missed.

Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text’s emotional tone ("sadly... I miss those days"). Distractor 1 confuses physical discomfort (headache) with fear. Distractor 2 reverses the wiring’s negative connotation. Distractor 3 misinterprets the noisy door as a positive memory.

Topic: a story about a talent show at my school

By Alex Chen

Every year, our school has a talent show, but this year’s was usual—it was crazy! The reporter from our school newspaper asked me to help. There was a toy desert full of prizes, like a small toy car, for the winners.

3. It was held outside. / Wrong: No location change is mentioned.

Distractors:
1. She quit because she was nervous. / Wrong: She "smiled and continued."
2. The audience laughed at her. / Wrong: No negative reaction is described.
3. She won the toy car. / Wrong: Prizes were general, not specified for Lily.

Distractors:
1. They stayed silent. / Wrong: Text says "shouted and clapped."
2. They asked for an encore. / Wrong: No mention of repeats.
3. They booed. / Wrong: No negative reaction is described.

Explanation:
The correct answer paraphrases "The audience shouted and clapped loudly." Distractor 1 is the opposite. Distractor 2 assumes professionalism. Distractor 3 is an unsupported inference.



Topic: a story about a surprise visit from an old friend

Text:
By Mark Rivera
Mark was surprised when his old friend, Jake, arrived at his door. It was noisy outside, but Jake waved and smiled. "I missed you!" he said. "Why didn’t you tell me you were coming?" Mark asked, laughing. Jake was wearing old pants and carrying a bag of nuts. "I lost a sock on the train," he added.

They sat in Mark’s living room and talked for hours. Jake told crazy stories about his new job. "You’re crazy!" Mark laughed. "But I love hearing them." They had a long discussion about old times. Later, they argued about music—Jake wanted loud songs, but Mark preferred quiet. "We have less time now," Jake said. "Let’s just enjoy it!" Mark agreed, and they stopped arguing.

As Jake left, he was driving too fast. "Be careful!" Mark shouted. "I’ll see you soon," Jake called back. They both smiled, happy to have reconnected.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Mark laugh when Jake said he lost a sock on the train?
Correct: Mark laughed because Jake’s surprise visit was unexpected.
Distractors:
1. Mark laughed because Jake’s sock was funny-looking.
2. Mark laughed because he was embarrassed about his own socks.
3. Mark laughed because Jake was telling a joke about nuts.
Explanation:
The text says, "Mark asked, laughing, ‘Why didn’t you tell me you were coming?’" The laugh is because Jake arrived unexpectedly, not because of the sock or nuts. A weak reader might focus on the sock or nuts and miss the context of Jake’s arrival.
---
Q2: What did Jake and Mark argue about during their discussion?
Correct: They argued about the type of music they should listen to.
Distractors:
1. They argued about Jake’s driving.
2. They argued about old jobs.
3. They argued about the bag of nuts.
Explanation:
The text states, "Later, they argued about music—Jake wanted loud songs, but Mark preferred quiet." Driving is mentioned only at the end, and the nuts/jobs are not part of the argument. A weak reader might confuse the argument topic with other details in the story.
---
Q3: How did the story end?
Correct: They said goodbye happily, promising to meet again soon.
Distractors:
1. Jake left angrily after their argument.
2. Mark chased Jake’s car to stop him from driving fast.
3. Jake forgot to say goodbye when he left.
Explanation:
The text ends with, "They both smiled, happy to have reconnected." Jake’s departure is friendly ("I’ll see you soon"), not angry or silent. A weak reader might focus on the driving scene and assume conflict or forgetfulness.

Topic: a simple piece of advice my father gave me

Text:
My father worked near a railway track in a quiet valley. One day, he gave me some advice that I still remember. He hated advertising because it often showed things that weren’t real. Instead, he gave me a spoon as equipment for life. "Use this," he said, "like the moon guiding the night—steady and true."

He also told me to always wear my uniform with pride, whether at school or work. "People notice how you behave," he said. "Treat others with oil—smoothly, without friction." His words were simple, but they helped me in many ways. Even now, I think of his advice when I face challenges.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the father dislike advertising?
Correct: Because he thought it often showed unrealistic things.
Distractors:
1. Because he worked near a railway track and found it noisy.
2. Because he believed it wasted money.
3. Because he preferred reading books instead.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text: "He hated advertising because it often showed things that weren’t real." Distractor 1 is wrong because the text does not link noise to advertising. Distractor 2 is unsupported—money is not mentioned. Distractor 3 is a common assumption but not stated in the text.
---
Q2: What did the spoon represent in his advice?
Correct: It was a symbol of practicality and reliability in life.
Distractors:
1. It was a tool to eat with every day.
2. It reminded him of cooking lessons.
3. It was a gift to show love.
Explanation:
The correct answer comes from "He gave me a spoon as equipment for life... steady and true." Distractor 1 is too literal—the spoon’s symbolic meaning is key. Distractor 2 is unsupported by the text. Distractor 3 is emotionally plausible but not mentioned.
---
Q3: How did the father want his son to treat others?
Correct: He wanted him to be kind and smooth in interactions.
Distractors:
1. He wanted him to always wear a uniform.
2. He wanted him to avoid conflicts at all costs.
3. He wanted him to speak loudly to be heard.
Explanation:
The correct answer is paraphrased from "Treat others with oil—smoothly, without friction." Distractor 1 confuses uniforms (mentioned earlier) with behavior. Distractor 2 is too extreme—the text suggests smoothness, not complete avoidance. Distractor 3 is unrelated to the text.



Topic: a story about a small thing that made me smile

Text:
By Maria Lopez

I was in the school library yesterday when I saw a chair fall over. It was broken, and I thought it was impossible to repair it. Firstly, I looked for the leader of the library, but she was busy. Then I saw a document on the desk with a system for fixing things. It was simple: just tighten the screws!

I tried, but the screws were too loose. Finally, a student named Tom helped me. He said, “Don’t worry, I can fix it!” He gave me an explanation of how to do it, and we worked together. The chair was good as new! It was a small thing, but it made me smile all day.

Questions:
Q1: What was the first thing Maria tried to do when she saw the broken chair?
Correct: She looked for the library leader to ask for help.
Distractors:
1. She tried to fix it herself immediately.
2. She called a repair technician.
3. She left the chair and walked away.

Explanation:
The text says, “Firstly, I looked for the leader of the library.” Maria didn’t try fixing it alone or call anyone—she first searched for help.
---
Q2: Why did Maria think the chair was impossible to fix at first?
Correct: She didn’t know how to repair it and thought it was too difficult.
Distractors:
1. She was afraid of breaking more things.
2. The chair was too heavy to move.
3. She didn’t have the right tools.

Explanation:
The text states, “I thought it was impossible to repair it.” There’s no mention of fear, weight, or missing tools—just uncertainty about fixing it.
---
Q3: How did Tom help Maria fix the chair?
Correct: He showed her how to tighten the screws and gave her instructions.
Distractors:
1. He brought a new chair to replace it.
2. He called someone to fix it for her.
3. He carried the chair outside to throw it away.

Explanation:
The text says, “He gave me an explanation of how to do it.” Tom didn’t replace the chair, call anyone, or throw it away—he guided Maria through the repair.



Topic: a story about a parrot that could copy voices

Text:
By Maria Lopez

Mr. Green had a small parrot that could copy voices. At first, he kept this secret because he thought people would not believe him. The parrot lived in a cage near a window, where light helped it see better.

One day, a thief stole a small gold coin from Mr. Green’s house. The thief ran into the forest, but the parrot heard him. It was physical strong for its size, so it flew after him. The parrot copied the thief’s voice and scared him alone in the dark.

Mr. Green was happy with his parrot’s progress. He even borrowed tools from a neighbor to make a bigger cage. Now, he is proud to show his talking bird to everyone!

Questions:
Q1: Why did Mr. Green keep his parrot’s ability a secret at first?
Correct: He didn’t want people to think he was lying.
Distractors:
1. He was afraid the parrot would escape.
2. The parrot was too noisy for his neighbors.
3. He thought the parrot’s ability was dangerous.
Explanation:
The text says Mr. Green "thought people would not believe him," so the correct answer matches this idea. Distractor 1 is wrong because escape isn’t mentioned; Distractor 2 is wrong because noise isn’t the issue; Distractor 3 is wrong because danger isn’t discussed. A weak reader might pick Distractor 1 because "secret" can sound risky, but the text clearly states disbelief.
---
Q2: How did the parrot help Mr. Green find the stolen gold coin?
Correct: It flew after the thief and copied his voice to frighten him.
Distractors:
1. It led Mr. Green to the forest where the thief hid.
2. It bit the thief until he dropped the coin.
3. It called the police by copying their voices.
Explanation:
The text describes the parrot "flew after him" and "copied the thief’s voice," so the correct answer fits. Distractor 1 is wrong because the parrot acted alone, not by guiding Mr. Green. Distractor 2 is wrong because the parrot used its voice, not its beak. Distractor 3 is wrong because police aren’t mentioned. A weak reader might pick Distractor 1 because "forest" is in the text, but the parrot’s role is copying voices.
---
Q3: What did Mr. Green do after the parrot scared the thief?
Correct: He made the parrot’s cage bigger with tools he borrowed from a neighbor.
Distractors:
1. He gave the parrot more food because it was hungry.
2. He sold the gold coin to buy a new cage.
3. He took the parrot to the vet because it was sick.
Explanation:
The text says Mr. Green "borrowed tools... to make a bigger cage," so the correct answer matches. Distractor 1 is wrong because hunger isn’t mentioned. Distractor 2 is wrong because the coin wasn’t sold. Distractor 3 is wrong because the parrot wasn’t sick. A weak reader might pick Distractor 1 because "happy" could imply reward, but the text specifies tools for a bigger cage.



Topic: a story about a stranger who helped me when I was lost

Text:
By Maria Lopez

Last summer, I was lost in a big city. It was my first time there, and I didn’t know the streets. Suddenly, a social man in a red jacket stopped and asked, "Do you need help?" He was very kind and gave me specific directions to my hotel.

When we arrived, I saw a small stage near a park. "I perform there every evening," he said. I wanted to receive a gift for his help—a small gold ring—but he smiled and said, "Perhaps next time." He carried a heavy wooden box, and I wondered what was inside.

A cold blow of wind made me shiver. Looking back, I saw him walking into the crowd, playing a guitar. I felt lucky to meet such a kind stranger.

Questions:
Q1: Why did Maria feel lucky at the end of the story?
Correct: She was grateful to meet someone kind in a big, unfamiliar place.
Distractors:
1. She found her way home without help.
2. The stranger gave her money for the bus.
3. She saw her friend in the crowd.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text ("felt lucky to meet such a kind stranger"). Distractor 1 is wrong because Maria needed help and got directions. Distractor 2 is wrong because no money was mentioned. Distractor 3 is wrong because she saw the stranger playing guitar, not a friend.
---
Q2: What did Maria want to give the stranger as a thank-you?
Correct: A small piece of jewelry made of gold.
Distractors:
1. A wooden box like his.
2. A ticket to his concert.
3. Flowers from the park.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text ("a small gold ring"). Distractor 1 is wrong because she wanted to give, not take his box. Distractor 2 is wrong because no concert tickets were mentioned. Distractor 3 is wrong because no flowers were mentioned.
---
Q3: What did Maria notice about the stranger’s box?
Correct: It was made of wood and seemed heavy.
Distractors:
1. It was empty.
2. It had gold inside.
3. It was broken.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text ("a heavy wooden box"). Distractor 1 is wrong because it was heavy, implying it was not empty. Distractor 2 is wrong because no gold was mentioned inside. Distractor 3 is wrong because no damage was described.



Topic: a fairy tale about a clever princess who solves puzzles to save her kingdom

Text:
By Princess Elara

I am a teenage princess, and my kingdom is in danger! The king is weak because of a curse. I noticed a small diary in the castle library with a puzzle inside. A wise old man gave me a tip: "Find the stone in the position where the moon shines at midnight."

I traveled a great distance to the old tower. The guards moved at a slow rate, so I had time. I kept a small stone in my pocket as a reminder. Using the method from the diary, I solved the puzzle and broke the curse!

Questions:
Q1: Why was the king weak?
Correct: Because of a curse.
Distractors:
1. Because he was old. / 2. Because he was sick. / 3. Because he lost a battle.
Explanation: The text clearly states "The king is weak because of a curse." The other options are false because the text does not mention age, illness, or battles.
---
Q2: What did the wise old man tell Princess Elara to do?
Correct: Find a stone where the moon shines at midnight.
Distractors:
1. Find a key under the tower. / 2. Wait until the guards leave. / 3. Read the diary carefully.
Explanation: The text says, "Find the stone in the position where the moon shines at midnight." The other options are false because they change key details from the text.
---
Q3: How did the guards help Princess Elara?
Correct: They moved slowly, giving her time to escape.
Distractors:
1. They let her pass without asking questions. / 2. They gave her a map. / 3. They were asleep.
Explanation: The text says, "The guards moved at a slow rate, so I had time." The other options are false because they contradict the text’s description of the guards’ behavior.



Topic: a fairy tale about tiny people

Text:
The Tiny People of Willow Wood

In a quiet wood, there lived tiny people who were very clever but very small. Their goal was to find clean water, but their home was always dry. "We must do something!" they said.

First, they tried to apply magic to make rain, but it did not work. Secondly, they decided to light a fire to boil water, but one of them burned his finger and cried in pain. "Ouch! That hurts!" he shouted.

Then, they found an old diary under a tree. Inside was a map to a hidden spring! They were surprised by their luck. A kind sir who lived nearby had written it years ago. "Thank you, sir!" they cheered.

Now, they have water every day, and their wood is no longer dry.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the tiny people need to find water?
Correct: Their home was always dry.
Distractors:
- They wanted to build a lake in the forest.
- They lost their old water source in a storm.
- They needed water for their magic spells.
Explanation:
The text says, "their home was always dry." The correct answer matches this. The other options are wrong because:
- Building a lake is not mentioned.
- No storm or lost source is described.
- Magic is tried but not for water specifically.
---
Q2: What happened when they tried to light a fire?
Correct: One of them burned his finger.
Distractors:
- The fire spread too quickly and burned the wood.
- They could not light the fire at all.
- They used the fire to cook food instead.
Explanation:
The text says, "one of them burned his finger." The correct answer is accurate. The others are wrong because:
- The fire did not burn the wood.
- They did light the fire (but got hurt).
- No cooking is mentioned.
---
Q3: How did the tiny people finally solve their problem?
Correct: They discovered a hidden spring using a map.
Distractors:
- The kind sir gave them water directly.
- They dug a well in the forest.
- They waited for rain to come naturally.
Explanation:
The text says, "they found an old diary... with a map to a hidden spring." The correct answer matches this. The others are wrong because:
- The sir only wrote the diary, not gave water.
- No digging is mentioned.
- They took action, not waited.



Topic: a fairy tale about a dragon who was evil, but became kind

Text:
The Dragon’s Secret

Long ago, a terrible dragon lived in a high mountain. Its breath had a strange smell, and villagers shut their doors when it flew near. The dragon was independent—it never asked for help. One day, a divorced pilot named Leo flew over the mountain. He saw a narrow path leading to a cave with a secret code on the door.

Leo was brave. He climbed up and knocked. The dragon opened one eye. “Who are you?” it asked. Leo tried to communicate. “I just want to talk,” he said. The dragon listened. It had been lonely for years.

Slowly, the dragon changed. It let Leo borrow a book and even gave him a tour of its lair. Soon, the villagers stopped running. The dragon was no longer evil—it was kind.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the villagers fear the dragon at first?
Correct: Because its breath smelled bad and it looked scary.
Distractors:
1. It stole food from their homes.
2. It had a secret code that hurt people.
3. It was divorced and angry at the world.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches the text’s description of the dragon’s smell and the villagers’ reaction (shut their doors). Distractor 1 is wrong because the text never mentions stolen food. Distractor 2 is wrong because the code was on the cave door, not a threat. Distractor 3 is wrong because Leo was divorced, not the dragon.
---
Q2: What did Leo do that surprised the dragon?
Correct: He tried to talk to it instead of running away.
Distractors:
1. He borrowed a book from the dragon.
2. He flew into the dragon’s cave without permission.
3. He used the secret code to enter.
Explanation:
The correct answer comes from “Leo tried to communicate” and “I just want to talk.” Distractor 1 is wrong because Leo borrowed the book after talking. Distractor 2 is wrong because the dragon opened one eye when Leo knocked—it didn’t stop him. Distractor 3 is wrong because the text doesn’t say Leo used the code.
---
Q3: How did the dragon’s behavior change at the end?
Correct: It became friendly and showed people its cave.
Distractors:
1. It learned to fly like a pilot.
2. It shut itself in the cave forever.
3. It gave tours only to divorced people.
Explanation:
The correct answer matches “the dragon was no longer evil—it was kind” and “gave him a tour.” Distractor 1 is wrong because the dragon didn’t learn to fly like Leo. Distractor 2 is wrong because the dragon opened to Leo, not shut itself away. Distractor 3 is wrong because the text doesn’t restrict tours to divorced people.



Topic: a fairy tale about a tree that grows golden coins, but only a kind person can take them

Text:
In a small village, there was a magic tree that grew golden coins. But only kind people could take them. The whole population relied on the tree for food and money.

One day, a professor visited and took a photograph of it. He said, “This tree teaches learning!” A greedy man tried to take too many coins, but they turned to leaves. Then, a child helped a lost bear, and the tree gave her a coin. After that, it stopped giving coins any more.

In autumn, the leaves fall, and the villagers check the tree every year. One man even climbed it in his pants—but he wasn’t kind, so nothing happened!

Questions:
Q1: Why did the tree stop giving golden coins?
Correct: The tree stopped giving coins because only kind people could take them, and no one else was kind after the child’s act.
Distractors:
- The professor took too many coins. / The tree’s leaves fell and blocked the coins. / The villagers stopped believing in magic.
Explanation:
The text says the tree gave coins only to kind people and stopped after the child’s act. The professor didn’t take coins, leaves falling is seasonal, and villagers’ beliefs aren’t mentioned. A weak reader might focus on the professor or fall without linking it to the coins.
---
Q2: What did the greedy man do that made the tree punish him?
Correct: He tried to take too many coins at once.
Distractors:
- He climbed the tree in his pants. / He didn’t share his coins. / He took coins after autumn.
Explanation:
The text states the greedy man’s punishment was for taking too many coins. Climbing in pants was unrelated, sharing isn’t mentioned, and autumn isn’t the time of punishment. A weak reader might confuse the pants detail or misread fall as a time reference.
---
Q3: How did the child prove she was kind?
Correct: She helped a lost bear.
Distractors:
- She took a photograph of the tree. / She checked the tree every year. / She wore pants while climbing.
Explanation:
The text clearly says the child helped the bear. The photograph was by the professor, checking the tree was the villagers’ habit, and pants were unrelated to the child. A weak reader might mix up actions or characters.



Topic: an adventure story about pirates

Text:
By Jack Sparrow

The pirates had an enormous ship, but it needed repair. Their captain wanted to connect two islands to find hidden treasure. One day, a passenger on the ship helped them identify a clue written in soap!

A major storm appeared, but the crew laughed and used the soap to make bubbles. Finally, they reached the treasure island and became rich!

Questions:
Q1: Why did the pirates need to connect the two islands?
Correct: To find hidden treasure.
Distractors:
1. To escape from enemies.
2. To build a new ship.
3. To visit a friend on the other island.
Explanation:
The text says, "Their captain wanted to connect two islands to find hidden treasure." The correct answer is "To find hidden treasure." The other options are wrong because the text does not mention enemies, building a new ship, or visiting a friend.
---
Q2: What did the passenger help the pirates do?
Correct: They helped identify a clue written in soap.
Distractors:
1. They helped steer the ship.
2. They gave them a map.
3. They fixed the broken ropes.
Explanation:
The text says, "A passenger on the ship helped them identify a clue written in soap." The correct answer is "They helped identify a clue written in soap." The other options are wrong because the text does not mention steering, a map, or fixing ropes.
---
Q3: How did the pirates survive the major storm?
Correct: They used soap to make bubbles.
Distractors:
1. They hid in the treasure chest.
2. They sailed away quickly.
3. They used a magic spell.
Explanation:
The text says, "A major storm appeared, but the crew laughed and used the soap to make bubbles." The correct answer is "They used soap to make bubbles." The other options are wrong because the text does not mention a treasure chest, sailing away, or magic.



Topic: a story about a school sports day

Text:
By Jamie Carter

Our school had a big Sports Day last week! The teachers moved all the furniture outside, and we stored the extra chairs underground near the gym. I was going to lead the 100-meter race, but just before it started, I dropped my rubbish wrapper on the track. Everyone laughed!

A strong blow of wind almost made a book with the race results fly away! Then, in the relay, my friend Tom passed the baton to me, but he got a terrible headache from the sun. "I need water!" he said.

At the end, Jake joked that he drank a drug to run faster. We all laughed—it was just his little brother’s book cover! There was even a bit of blood when Lucy scraped her knee, but she didn’t stop running.

It was the best Sports Day ever!

Questions:
Q1: Why did Jamie almost drop the race results?
Correct: Because a strong wind almost blew the book away.
Distractors:
1. Because he tripped over his shoelaces.
2. Because someone stole the book.
3. Because the book was too heavy.
Explanation:
The text says, "A strong blow of wind almost made a book with the race results fly away!"
- Correct: Matches the wind detail.
- Distractor 1: Wrong because the text mentions wind, not tripping.
- Distractor 2: Wrong because the text doesn’t mention theft.
- Distractor 3: Wrong because the issue is the wind, not weight.
---
Q2: What did Jake pretend to drink to run faster?
Correct: He pretended to drink an energy drug.
Distractors:
1. He pretended to drink water.
2. He pretended to drink juice.
3. He pretended to drink coffee.
Explanation:
The text says, "Jake joked that he drank a drug to run faster."
- Correct: Matches the drug detail (even as a joke).
- Distractor 1: Wrong because the text specifies drug, not water.
- Distractor 2: Wrong because juice isn’t mentioned.
- Distractor 3: Wrong because coffee isn’t mentioned.
---
Q3: How did Tom feel during the relay race?
Correct: He felt unwell because he had a headache.
Distractors:
1. He felt tired because he ran too fast.
2. He felt happy because he passed the baton well.
3. He felt scared because of the crowd.
Explanation:
The text says, "Tom passed the baton to me, but he got a terrible headache from the sun."
- Correct: Matches the headache detail.
- Distractor 1: Wrong because the text mentions a headache, not tiredness.
- Distractor 2: Wrong because the text focuses on his discomfort, not happiness.
- Distractor 3: Wrong because fear isn’t mentioned.



Topic: a story about my favorite holiday memory from when I was a child

Text:
My Favorite Holiday
When I was a child, I loved holidays. One summer, I saw an advertisement for a beach trip with my family. The heat was terrible, but I was excited! My task was to pack my bag with swimsuits and toys. My parents helped, and we left early in the morning.

At the beach, there was a variety of activities: swimming, sandcastles, and games. The noise of the crowd was exciting, but I said, "Wow!" when I saw the big waves. Everyone was happy except my little brother, who fell into the water. Sadly, he cried, but we laughed and helped him.

As autumn fell, I remembered that holiday. We lived close to the beach, so we visited often. It was my favorite memory, even with the small problems.

Questions:
Q1: What was the child’s task before the holiday?
Correct: He had to pack his bag with swimsuits and toys.
Distractors:
1. Help his parents pack the car. / Wrong: The text specifies his task was packing his own bag.
2. Swim in the ocean every day. / Wrong: Swimming was an activity during the holiday, not a preparation task.
3. Watch an advertisement on TV. / Wrong: The advertisement was seen before packing, not the task itself.
Explanation:
The text says, "My task was to pack my bag with swimsuits and toys." The correct answer matches this exactly. Distractor 1 is wrong because the child’s task was individual (his bag), not helping parents. Distractor 2 is wrong because swimming was an activity at the holiday, not a task before it. Distractor 3 is wrong because the advertisement was seen earlier, not the task.
---
Q2: Why did the child’s brother cry at the beach?
Correct: He fell into the water.
Distractors:
1. He was too hot in the sun. / Wrong: The heat is mentioned but linked to the child’s excitement, not the brother’s crying.
2. He didn’t like the noise of the crowd. / Wrong: The noise is described as exciting, not upsetting.
3. He lost his toys in the sand. / Wrong: The text doesn’t mention lost toys; the brother cried because he fell into the water.
Explanation:
The text states, "my little brother, who fell into the water." The correct answer paraphrases this. Distractor 1 is wrong because the heat is described as terrible for the child, not the brother. Distractor 2 is wrong because the noise is exciting. Distractor 3 is wrong because toys aren’t mentioned as a cause for crying.
---
Q3: How did the child feel about the holiday, even though something went wrong?
Correct: He still thought it was his favorite memory because the good parts were more important.
Distractors:
1. He was sad because his brother ruined the fun. / Wrong: The text says they laughed and helped him, showing it wasn’t ruined.
2. He forgot about the holiday after it ended. / Wrong: The text says he remembered it fondly in autumn.
3. He wished they had gone somewhere colder. / Wrong: The text doesn’t mention wishing for a colder place; the heat was just part of the experience.
Explanation:
The text says, "It was my favorite memory, even with the small problems." The correct answer paraphrases this. Distractor 1 is wrong because the brother’s crying was resolved with laughter. Distractor 2 is wrong because the child remembered the holiday. Distractor 3 is wrong because the text doesn’t mention a desire for colder weather.



Topic: a story about visiting a zoo

Text:
By Emma White

Last weekend, my family visited the zoo. The weather was sunny, and we saw many animals that looked alive and happy. The zoo manager told us that they earn money by selling tickets and food. He said they take carefully care of all the animals.

My little brother was extremely excited. He wanted to see either the lions or the monkeys first. We watched the lions roar and the monkeys jump. Then, we saw birds singing on a small stage. My brother laughed and said, "The elephant looks like a big sock!"

The zoo was big, but the animals were able to move freely. We had a great time and promised to come back soon.

Questions:
Q1: Why did the zoo manager talk to the family?
Correct: To explain how the zoo makes money and takes care of animals.
Distractors:
1. To tell them about the zoo’s history.
2. To ask if they wanted to buy tickets.
3. To warn them about dangerous animals.
Explanation:
The text says the manager explained how the zoo earns money and cares for animals. The correct answer matches this. Distractor 1 is wrong because history is not mentioned. Distractor 2 is wrong because he did not ask about tickets. Distractor 3 is wrong because the animals looked happy, not dangerous.
---
Q2: What did the little brother think the elephant looked like?
Correct: Like a big sock.
Distractors:
1. Like a big ball.
2. Like a gray cloud.
3. Like a sleeping bear.
Explanation:
The text directly quotes the brother saying, "The elephant looks like a big sock!" Distractor 1 is wrong because the text does not mention a ball. Distractor 2 is wrong because the elephant was not compared to a cloud. Distractor 3 is wrong because the text does not mention a bear.
---
Q3: How did the family feel about their visit?
Correct: They enjoyed it very much and wanted to return.
Distractors:
1. They were bored and left early.
2. They only liked the singing birds.
3. They felt the zoo was too small.
Explanation:
The text says, "We had a great time and promised to come back soon." The correct answer matches this. Distractor 1 is wrong because the family had a great time. Distractor 2 is wrong because they enjoyed all the animals, not just the birds. Distractor 3 is wrong because the zoo was described as big.



Topic: a story about a picnic in the park

Text:
A Picnic in the Park
By Emma Carter

Last summer, my family used to go to Green Park for picnics. The park was always tidy, and we loved sitting under the big oak tree. This year, we decided to go again.

I packed a plastic plate, some sandwiches, and a lemon cake. My brother brought his digital camera, but Mum said, "No phones or cameras today!" We wanted to enjoy the sense of nature.

When we arrived, there were nowhere to sit because all the tables were full. So, we ate easily on the grass. My little sister laughed and said, "This is the best picnic ever!" Her comment made us all smile.

Later, we talked about next time. "We’ll review our picnic plans," Dad said. "Maybe we’ll bring a blanket!"

Questions:
Q1: Why did Emma’s family decide to go to Green Park again this year?
Correct: They had enjoyed going there before and wanted to do it again.
Distractors:
1. They had never been before and wanted to try it.
2. The park was cleaner than other parks.
3. Their friends invited them.
Explanation:
The text says, "Last summer... we used to go," showing it was a habit. The correct answer matches this. Distractor 1 misinterprets "used to" as a first-time visit. Distractor 2 ignores the reason for returning (nostalgia, not cleanliness). Distractor 3 is unsupported by the text.
---
Q2: What rule did Mum make about electronic devices during the picnic?
Correct: They weren’t allowed to use phones or cameras.
Distractors:
1. They had to turn off their phones.
2. Only cameras were allowed.
3. They could use tablets but not phones.
Explanation:
The text states, "No phones or cameras today!" The correct answer paraphrases this. Distractor 1 is partially correct but misses "cameras." Distractor 2 contradicts the text (both were banned). Distractor 3 introduces unsupported devices ("tablets").
---
Q3: What did Emma’s little sister say that made everyone smile?
Correct: She said it was the best picnic ever.
Distractors:
1. She said the lemon cake was delicious.
2. She asked if they could go again next week.
3. She complained about sitting on the grass.
Explanation:
The text says, "‘This is the best picnic ever!’ Her comment made us all smile." The correct answer paraphrases this. Distractor 1 is plausible but unsupported (no comment on taste). Distractor 2 confuses future plans ("next time") with her immediate remark. Distractor 3 contradicts her happy tone.



Topic: a story about a time I was dressed completely wrong for the weather

Text:
"One day, I decided to walk to the park. I thought it was necessary to wear a thick coat, gloves, and a hat because the weather app said it would be very cold. I slowly left my house, but after five minutes, I started to feel too hot. I stopped and looked at my clothes. I slowly recognized my mistake—I had dressed for winter, but it was actually a warm spring day!

My phone provided an electronic guide, but it had the wrong information. I touched my sweaty hands and realized how uncomfortable I was. I held my coat in my arms for a while, but I couldn’t finish my walk in it. People invented these apps to help us, but sometimes they don’t work!

I went home quickly and changed into lighter clothes. Now I always check the weather twice!"

Questions:
Q1: Why did the narrator wear a thick coat?
Correct: Because they thought the weather would be very cold.
Distractors:
1. They wanted to look fashionable.
2. Their friend told them to.
3. They forgot to check the weather.
Explanation: The text says, "I thought it was necessary to wear a thick coat... because the weather app said it would be very cold." The correct answer matches this. The distractors are wrong because:
- Fashion is not mentioned.
- No friend is mentioned.
- The narrator did check the weather, but the app was wrong.
---

Correct: They were too hot because they had dressed for winter instead of spring.
Distractors:
1. They had left their keys at home.
2. The park was closed.
3. Their shoes were uncomfortable.
Explanation: The text says, "I started to feel too hot... I slowly recognized my mistake—I had dressed for winter, but it was actually a warm spring day!" The correct answer matches this. The distractors are wrong because:
- Keys are not mentioned.
- The park’s status is not discussed.
- The issue was clothing, not shoes.
---
Q3: Why did the narrator go home quickly?
Correct: Because they were uncomfortable in their heavy clothes.
Distractors:
1. They saw a friend and wanted to visit.
2. It started to rain.
3. They remembered an important call.
Explanation: The text says, "I couldn’t finish my walk in it [the coat]... I went home quickly and changed into lighter clothes." The correct answer matches this. The distractors are wrong because:
- No friend is mentioned.
- Rain is not mentioned.
